# SWM (Spherical World Model) Experiment Log

## Summary

Goal: spherical representations (S^{d-1}) + non-SIGReg anti-collapse for world models (plan.md / plan_v2.md V0).

Base config: SphericalJEPA, ViT-Tiny, embed_dim=64, batch_size=128, T=4, Two-Room, lr=5e-5, spread weight=0.1.

### Uniformity_loss scale reference (t=2, N=512)

| State | uniformity_loss value | Meaning |
|-------|----------------------|---------|
| Full collapse (all z_i identical) | **6.24** = log(511) | All sq_dist=0, exp(0)=1 |
| Random 64D unit vectors | **≈2.2** = log(511·e⁻⁴) | avg sq_dist≈2 |
| Well-spread | **< 2.2** | avg sq_dist > 2 |

### Mean cosine spread_loss scale reference

| State | spread_loss value |
|-------|------------------|
| Full collapse | **1.0** (all cosine sim = 1) |
| Random 64D unit vectors | **≈0.0** |

### Results overview

| # | Approach | Loss type | Init spread | E0 fit spread | E0 val spread | Collapse broken? | Failure mode |
|---|----------|-----------|-------------|--------------|---------------|-----------------|--------------|
| 1 | Linear + mean cosine | cosine | 1.0 | 1.0 | 1.0 | No | Gradient dead zone |
| 2 | + detach target | cosine | 1.0 | 1.0 | 1.0 | No | Gradient dead zone |
| 3 | InfoNCE (τ=0.1) | InfoNCE | 16.24 | 16.24→7.55(E8) | 7.57 | E1 violent break | Destroys temporal structure |
| 4 | MLP+BN projector | cosine | 1.0 | **0.006** | **1.0** | Train only | Batch masking |
| 5 | + LayerNorm pre-L2 | cosine | 1.0 | 1.0 | 1.0 | No | Gradient dead zone |
| 6 | + noise (σ=1e-2) | cosine | 1.0 | 1.0 | 1.0 | No | Gradient dead zone |
| 7 | variance_loss on emb_raw | variance | 0.996 | 1.0 | 1.0 | No | Gradient dead zone (0/0) |
| 8 | DINO centering + uniformity | uniformity | 6.24 | **3.94** | **6.24** | Train only | Batch masking |
| 9 | uniformity_loss only (no centering) | uniformity | 6.24 | 6.24 | 6.23 | No | Gradient dead zone |

### Three failure modes

| Mode | Experiments | Mechanism |
|------|------------|-----------|
| **Gradient dead zone** | 1, 2, 5, 6, 7, 9 | At collapse all z_i identical → (z_i−z_j)=0, std=0/0 → zero gradient |
| **Batch-dependent masking** | 4, 8 | BN / centering creates diversity during training only → loss gets no corrective signal → eval reveals true collapse |
| **Temporal destruction** | 3 | InfoNCE pushes ALL pairs apart (including adjacent frames) → conflicts with pred_loss |

### Root cause

Random ViT CLS tokens = shared component (v, magnitude ~10) + input-dependent variation (ε_i, magnitude ~0.01). L2 normalisation maps v+ε_i to v/||v|| ≈ same unit vector for all i, killing the ε_i signal. This is unique to spherical architectures — LeWM in R^d preserves ε_i and SIGReg's sorting mechanism detects it.

### What works (SIGReg) and why

SIGReg succeeds because of **sorting + quantile matching** inside Epps-Pulley: project embeddings to random 1D direction → sort → compare sorted values against Gaussian characteristic function. Sorting assigns different ranks to identical values, providing non-zero gradient at exact collapse. No batch-dependence, no pairwise differences.

---

## Experiment Details

### Exp 1: V0 Baseline (Linear + mean cosine spread_loss)

**Config**: `nn.Linear(hidden_dim, embed_dim)` projector, spread_loss = mean pairwise cosine, weight=0.1.

| Stage | pred_loss | spread_loss | loss |
|-------|-----------|-------------|------|
| Init | 1.061 | 1.0 | 1.162 |
| E3 fit | 2.1e-5 | 1.0 | 0.100 |
| E3 val | 2.6e-5 | 1.0 | 0.100 |

Complete collapse. Predictor trivially outputs constant.

---

### Exp 2: + detach() on target

**Change**: `tgt_emb = emb[:, n_preds:].detach()`.

No improvement. Collapse is in the encoder, not gradient flow through target.

---

### Exp 3: InfoNCE spread_loss (τ=0.1)

**Change**: `logsumexp(sim/τ)`. InfoNCE scale: collapse=16.24, random=6.24.

| Stage | pred_loss | spread_loss |
|-------|-----------|-------------|
| Init | 1.080 | 16.236 (collapse) |
| E0 fit | 0.001 | 16.236 (still collapsed) |
| E1 fit | 0.966 | 9.286 (broke out) |
| E8 fit | 0.875 | 7.551 (near random) |
| E8 val | 0.879 | 7.573 |

Broke collapse at E1 via float noise accumulation, but pred_loss jumped to untrained level (0.97) and barely recovered. InfoNCE pushes temporal neighbours apart, directly opposing pred_loss.

---

### Exp 4: MLP projector with BatchNorm

**Change**: `MLP(hidden_dim, 2048, embed_dim, norm_fn=BatchNorm1d)`.

| Stage | pred_loss | spread_loss (cosine) |
|-------|-----------|---------------------|
| Init | 0.935 | 1.0 |
| E0 fit | 0.049 | **0.006** |
| E0 val | 0.017 | **1.0** |

Train/val split: BN decorrelates during training (batch stats) → spread sees no collapse → no corrective gradient. Eval with running stats → collapse exposed.

---

### Exp 5: LayerNorm before L2 normalize

| Stage | pred_loss | spread_loss (cosine) |
|-------|-----------|---------------------|
| Init | 1.104 | 1.0 |
| E0 fit | 4.5e-4 | 1.0 |

LayerNorm is per-sample. Doesn't fix cross-sample similarity.

---

### Exp 6: Noise injection (σ=1e-2)

| Stage | pred_loss | spread_loss (cosine) |
|-------|-----------|---------------------|
| Init | 0.880 | 1.0 |
| E0 fit | 6.0e-4 | 1.0 |

σ=1e-2 negligible vs embedding magnitude (~10). Angular perturbation ≈ 0.001 rad.

---

### Exp 7: variance_loss on pre-L2-norm embeddings

**Change**: `clamp(1 - std_per_dim, min=0).mean()` on emb_raw.

| Stage | pred_loss | spread_loss (variance) |
|-------|-----------|----------------------|
| Init | 1.138 | 0.996 |
| E0 fit | 7.9e-4 | 1.0 |

variance gradient = (x_i − mean)/(N·std) = 0/0 at collapse. PyTorch resolves to 0.

---

### Exp 8: DINO centering + uniformity_loss

**Change**: Subtract batch mean (train) / EMA center (eval) before L2 norm. uniformity_loss (t=2) on normalised embeddings.

| Stage | pred_loss | spread_loss (uniformity) |
|-------|-----------|--------------------------|
| Init | 1.053 | 6.236 (= collapse) |
| E0 fit | 0.986 | **3.935** (partially spread) |
| E0 val | 0.350 | **6.235** (= collapse) |

Same pattern as Exp 4: centering creates diversity during training (fit spread improved), but eval with EMA center still collapsed. Centering ≈ BN = batch-dependent operation that masks collapse from the loss.

---

### Exp 9: uniformity_loss only (no centering, Linear projector)

Isolate uniformity_loss without any architectural changes to confirm baseline behaviour.

| Stage | pred_loss | spread_loss (uniformity) |
|-------|-----------|--------------------------|
| Init | 0.932 | 6.236 (= collapse) |
| E0 fit | 6.2e-4 | 6.236 (= collapse) |
| E0 val | 8.3e-5 | 6.235 (= collapse) |

Complete collapse. uniformity_loss has zero gradient at collapse (same dead zone as other pairwise losses).

---

## Key Insight: The Gradient Dead Zone

At exact collapse (all z_i identical), gradient is zero for ALL tested losses:

| Loss type | Why zero at collapse |
|-----------|---------------------|
| Pairwise (cosine, uniformity, InfoNCE) | (z_i − z_j) = 0 |
| Statistical (variance, covariance) | (x_i − mean) = 0, std = 0 → 0/0 |
| Gram matrix on post-norm | Gradient ∝ (G−I)z₀, killed by L2 Jacobian (I−z₀z₀ᵀ)z₀ = 0 |

**Only mechanism known to work: sorting + quantile matching** (used inside SIGReg's Epps-Pulley). Sorting assigns different ranks to identical values → different target quantiles → non-zero gradient. Does not involve (z_i − z_j) or batch statistics.
```

# 球面世界模型：在 LeWM 基础上探索球面表征的最小实验方案

## Motivation

Joint-Embedding Predictive Architecture (JEPA) 系列的世界模型最近取得了显著进展，但仍存在一些值得探讨的问题。LeWorldModel (LeWM) 作为一个端到端、从像素直接训练的 JEPA 世界模型，用 prediction loss 加 SIGReg 两个简洁的损失项实现了竞争性的控制性能。但在 Two-Room 这类相对简单的环境上，LeWM 的成功率（87%）明显低于一些更朴素的方法（100%），这暗示其表征可能没有最优地反映状态空间的真实结构。

一个合理的假设是：**SIGReg 强制嵌入分布匹配各向同性高斯，对低内在维度的环境（如 Two-Room 的 2 维状态空间）是过度约束**。真实的状态流形被强行撑到高维的高斯形状，可能稀释了状态之间的拓扑关系。

另一个观察是：**球面表征在自监督学习领域已经是被反复验证的成熟选择**。从 SimCLR 到 CLIP 到 DINO，几乎所有现代对比学习方法都把表征归一化到单位球面上。这种选择的优势包括避免高维下的距离集中现象、训练稳定性、和余弦相似度的几何自然性。但球面表征在世界模型场景下的具体效果尚未被系统验证——LeWM 用的是欧氏空间，V-JEPA 2 也是欧氏空间。

本工作提出一个最小化的研究问题：**在世界模型场景下，把欧氏表征 + SIGReg 替换成球面表征 + 简单的对比损失，能否带来改进**？这个问题的答案不仅决定一个具体方法的成败，更重要的是验证一个核心假设——LeJEPA 证明的"各向同性高斯最优"结论是否适用于世界模型场景，还是只适用于静态表征学习场景。

## Background

### LeWorldModel 的核心机制

LeWM 由一个 ViT 编码器 $\text{enc}_\theta: \mathcal{O} \to \mathbb{R}^d$ 和一个 transformer 预测器 $\text{pred}_\phi: \mathbb{R}^d \times \mathcal{A} \to \mathbb{R}^d$ 组成。训练目标是：

$$\mathcal{L}_{\text{LeWM}} = \underbrace{\|\hat{z}_{t+1} - z_{t+1}\|^2}_{\text{预测损失}} + \lambda \cdot \underbrace{\text{SIGReg}(Z)}_{\text{分布形状约束}}$$

预测损失让 predictor 学习动力学。SIGReg 通过 Cramer-Wold 投影把"检验高维分布是否高斯"化归到一维上的 Epps-Pulley 检验，强制嵌入边缘分布匹配各向同性高斯。两个项缺一不可——没有预测损失表征无法反映动力学，没有 SIGReg 表征会塌缩到一点。

LeWM 的设计哲学是用最少的损失项实现稳定训练。整个方案只有一个 trade-off 超参数（SIGReg 的权重 $\lambda$），训练在单 GPU 上几小时内可以完成。

### LeJEPA 的理论框架

LeJEPA 证明在静态表征学习场景下，**各向同性高斯是 JEPA 嵌入分布的最优形式**。证明分两个部分：第一部分证明 covariance 必须是各向同性的（在固定 trace 约束下，这种 covariance 让线性 probe 的 bias 和 variance 都最小）；第二部分证明在固定二阶矩约束下，高斯是最大熵分布，因此让非线性 probe 的 integrated squared bias 最小。

这个定理的关键假设是下游任务是任意线性或核 probe、对所有方向均匀敏感、表征空间是 $\mathbb{R}^d$。这些假设在静态表征学习场景下是合理的（"任意下游任务"是合理的 worst-case），但在世界模型场景下不一定成立——世界模型的下游任务是规划，规划有具体的几何结构（追求"相邻状态有相似表征、远离状态有不同表征"），不是任意的。

LeJEPA 没有覆盖球面表征空间下的最优性问题。Field-JEPA 的论点不是反驳 LeJEPA，而是认为世界模型场景的下游任务结构让另一种几何（球面）成为可行选择，需要实证检验。

### 球面对比学习的成熟经验

球面表征在自监督学习中是被验证的成熟选择。SimCLR、MoCo、CLIP、DINO 等方法都把表征 $L^2$ 归一化到单位球面，使用余弦相似度作为度量，用 alignment（让正样本对齐）和 uniformity（让所有样本均匀分布在球面上）两个目标驱动训练。Wang & Isola 2020 的工作给出了这套框架的理论分析。

球面归一化的几个核心优势：避免高维空间的距离集中现象，让相似度度量在高维下保持区分度；提供天然的尺度约束，避免表征 magnitude 发散；和余弦相似度的兼容性让相似度计算便宜且数值稳定；紧致空间让"覆盖"和"分散"等概念有明确的几何意义。

V-JEPA 2 的潜在表征虽然不是显式的球面归一化，但分析显示它**自然形成 hypersphere pocket 结构**——不同概念以连续的方向变化而不是离散跳跃的形式编码。这暗示 JEPA 类方法可能本来就倾向于学到球面几何，只是没有显式地利用这一点。

## Method

### 核心想法

把 LeWM 的欧氏表征空间 $\mathbb{R}^d$ 替换为球面表征空间 $S^{d-1}$，把 SIGReg 替换为简单的对比形式损失。每个观测 $o$ 通过 encoder 被映射到球面上的一个点 $\mu(o) \in S^{d-1}$，predictor 预测下一时刻的球面点 $\hat{\mu}_{t+1}$。

这个修改保留 LeWM 的所有其他设计——同样的 ViT 编码器骨干、ViT-S 预测器、相同的训练数据、相同的下游 CEM 规划过程。唯一的变化是表征所住的几何空间和防塌缩机制。

### 架构

**Encoder**：保留 LeWM 的 ViT-Tiny 骨干结构。在原本输出 192 维向量的位置加一个 linear 投影到目标维度 $d$，然后做 $L^2$ 归一化得到 $\mu(o) \in S^{d-1}$。这个修改只增加一个线性层和一次归一化操作，其他完全不变。

**Predictor**：保留 LeWM 的 ViT-S 结构（包括动作通过 AdaLN 注入的机制）。输入和输出都从欧氏向量变成球面点——输入 $\mu_t \in S^{d-1}$，输出 $\hat{\mu}_{t+1} \in S^{d-1}$，输出端同样加 $L^2$ 归一化。

球面表征的维度 $d$ 是一个新的超参数，但它的语义和 LeWM 的嵌入维度对应——可以从 16 或类似的较小维度开始。

### 训练目标

总损失包含两项：

$$\mathcal{L} = \mathcal{L}_{\text{pred}} + \lambda \cdot \mathcal{L}_{\text{spread}}$$

**预测损失** 衡量 predictor 输出与下一时刻真实表征的方向差异。最简单的形式是余弦距离：

$$\mathcal{L}_{\text{pred}} = 1 - \cos(\hat{\mu}_{t+1}, \mu_{t+1})$$

由于 $\hat{\mu}$ 和 $\mu$ 都是单位向量，余弦就是它们的内积。最小化这个损失等价于让预测的方向和真实的方向尽可能对齐。

**Spread 损失** 防止表征塌缩。最简单的形式是 batch 内 pairwise 余弦相似度的平均：

$$\mathcal{L}_{\text{spread}} = \frac{1}{B(B-1)} \sum_{i \neq j} \mu_i \cdot \mu_j$$

最小化这个损失鼓励 batch 内不同观测的表征互相分散。它是 Wang & Isola 2020 中 uniformity loss 的一个简化形式，对应"让边缘分布在球面上尽可能 spread out"的目标。

**两项之间的张力**给出方案的核心机制。预测损失要求时间相邻的观测有相似表征（让 predictor 能学到动力学），spread 损失要求 batch 内不同观测的表征互相分散（防止塌缩）。这两个力的平衡点是：**时间相邻的观测对应球面上接近的点，时间远离或不相关的观测对应球面上不同的点**——这正是反映物理状态拓扑的表征。

只有一个 trade-off 超参数 $\lambda$，与 LeWM 的 SIGReg 权重数量相同。

### 实现的最简方案与可能的升级

**预测损失**：最简方案就是上面的余弦距离。这是一个数值稳定、计算便宜、广泛使用的损失形式，没有需要升级的地方。如果发现训练后期收敛缓慢，可以考虑加一个温度参数让梯度更 sharp。

**Spread 损失**：最简方案是 pairwise 余弦相似度的平均。这是一个可计算、可微、有正确…81 tokens truncated…max 操作，可以接受。InfoNCE 在大规模对比学习中是标准做法，超参数选择有大量经验。

另一个值得考虑的升级是用 Wang & Isola 2020 的 uniformity loss 的精确形式：

$$\mathcal{L}_{\text{uniform}} = \log \mathbb{E}[\exp(-t \cdot \|\mu_i - \mu_j\|^2)]$$

这是一个有理论支撑的 uniformity 度量，鼓励样本在球面上均匀分布（在 $t \to \infty$ 的极限下精确测量分布到均匀分布的距离）。

**球面归一化的实现细节**：直接的 $L^2$ normalize 可能在训练早期出现数值不稳定（如果输入向量接近零）。一个简单的修补是在归一化前加一个小常数：$\mu = z / (\|z\| + \epsilon)$，其中 $\epsilon$ 是个很小的数（比如 $10^{-8}$）。这是个标准技巧，没有副作用。

**Predictor 输出的归一化**：predictor 输出后做 $L^2$ normalize，确保 $\hat{\mu}$ 也在球面上。这让预测损失（余弦距离）的计算保持一致。

## Experiment Design

### 整体策略

实验分阶段进行，每个阶段验证一个具体假设。**第一阶段（最小实验 V0）是核心**——它独立验证"球面表征 + 简单对比损失"是否能在世界模型场景下匹配或超过 LeWM 的欧氏表征 + SIGReg。这个阶段的结果决定整个研究方向是否值得继续。

后续阶段是对 V0 的扩展——加入更复杂的元素（vMF 参数化、几何 OOD 边界）来探索是否能进一步改善性能或获得新的能力。后续阶段只在前一阶段成功时进行。

每个阶段的设计原则是**只引入一个变化**，让结果的归因清晰。如果某个阶段的结果显著变化，可以确定是这一步引入的元素的贡献。

### 阶段 1：最小实验 V0

**实现的方案**：上面 Method 部分描述的完整方案——球面 encoder 输出 + 球面 predictor + 余弦预测损失 + pairwise spread 损失。实现工作主要是修改 LeWM 的 encoder 和 predictor 接口、替换损失函数、移除 SIGReg。

**实验环境**：从 Two-Room 开始，因为这是 LeWM 的已知弱点环境（87% 成功率）。如果 V0 在 Two-Room 上能 work，再扩展到 PushT、OGBench-Cube、Reacher 这些 LeWM 的标准环境。

**对比基线**：原版 LeWM。两个版本使用完全相同的训练数据、相同的 architecture 骨干、相同的 CEM 规划设置，只在表征几何和损失函数上有差异。

**评估指标**：

主要指标是 **in-distribution 任务成功率**——和 LeWM 的评估协议完全一致（CEM planning，相同的 sample budget、iterations、horizon）。这是最直接的端到端性能度量，决定 V0 是否有 practical value。

次要指标是 **训练动态的稳定性**——loss 曲线、梯度范数、有效维度的演化。这些指标告诉我们球面表征在世界模型训练中的行为是否健康，是否有 LeWM 没有的训练稳定性问题。

诊断指标包括 **表征几何的可视化**——用 t-SNE 或 UMAP 把训练后的 $\mu(o)$ 投影到 2D，目视检查是否反映物理状态空间的结构。对 Two-Room，预期看到反映两个房间和门连接关系的 2D 流形。对 PushT，预期看到反映 agent 位置和 block 位置的子结构。这些可视化是验证"球面表征是否真的捕捉物理结构"的直接证据。

为了区分 signal 和 noise，每个版本应该跑多个 random seeds（典型是 3-5 个），报告均值和标准差。

**判断标准**：

- 如果 V0 的成功率明显高于或匹配 LeWM（在统计意义上没有劣势），且表征几何诊断显示物理结构得到反映——核心假设成立，进入阶段 2。
- 如果 V0 明显劣于 LeWM——核心假设不成立，停止后续阶段，把这个负面结果作为对 LeJEPA 在世界模型场景的扩展验证。
- 如果 V0 和 LeWM 性能相当但训练更稳定或更简洁——也算 partial success，球面方向有 implementation 优势但没有性能优势。

### 后续阶段的简要规划

**阶段 2：加入 vMF 参数化**。在 V0 基础上让 encoder 同时输出每个观测的集中度参数 $\kappa(o)$，每个观测从球面点变成 vMF 分布 $\rho(o) = \text{vMF}(\mu(o), \kappa(o))$。Predictor 也输出 $(\hat{\mu}, \hat{\kappa})$。预测损失从余弦距离扩展为同时包含方向差异和集中度差异。加入一个 sharp loss 鼓励 $\kappa$ 大（让每个分布集中而不是 spread out）。

这个阶段验证的假设是：**自适应分辨率**（不同观测对应不同确定性的分布）能否带来额外的性能提升。预期在物理状态空间有明显热点的环境上更有用。

**阶段 3：加入 center 约束**。在阶段 2 基础上引入一个可学习的虚拟中心 $\mu_0 \in S^{d-1}$，加一个 hinge loss 把所有训练数据的 $\mu$ 约束在以 $\mu_0$ 为中心、最大角距离为 $\theta_{\max}$ 的球冠内。这给出一个明确的"已知区域"的几何边界，球冠外的球面区域作为 OOD 区域。

这个阶段引入 **OOD 检测能力**作为方案的新特性。除了 in-distribution 性能，还需要新的评估——在 perturbed 数据（视觉变化、物理参数变化）上测试 OOD 检测的 recall 和 precision，并且要验证 in-distribution 性能不被 center 约束损害。

OOD 评估的具体设计包括三类测试：像素级 OOD（修改视觉属性如 agent 颜色），物理 OOD（修改环境参数如门位置），动力学 OOD（应用不可能的动作序列）。每类 OOD 都有不同的预期——像素 OOD 测试表征是否对视觉细节过度敏感，物理 OOD 测试 OOD 边界是否能捕捉训练分布之外的状态，动力学 OOD 测试 prediction error 是否能作为辅助 OOD 信号。

### 阶段之间的递进逻辑

阶段 1、2、3 形成一个 ladder。每个阶段在前一个的基础上只加一个元素，让贡献的归因清晰。

如果阶段 1 失败，stop。如果阶段 2 没有比阶段 1 进一步改善，那 vMF 参数化在这个场景下是不必要的复杂度，论文的核心贡献是 V0。如果阶段 3 没有进一步改善但 OOD 检测能力出现且 in-distribution 性能保持，那 center 约束是有价值的扩展，论文包含 V0 加 OOD 检测的两个独立贡献。

这个 ladder 的优势是**每一个 stop 点都是一个有意义的论文 framing**。即使最终只完成 V0，结果也能作为"球面世界模型"的初步验证发表。即使 vMF 没有改善 V0，"自适应分辨率在简单世界模型环境下的 negative result" 仍然是有信息量的发现。即使 center 约束没有改善 in-distribution 性能但提供了 OOD 检测能力，这本身就是一个有价值的功能扩展。

### 一个执行上的原则

整个实验过程遵循一个原则：**先用最简方案跑通，再决定是否升级**。每一次升级都应该由具体的失败模式触发——发现训练不稳定时考虑升级 spread loss 到 InfoNCE 形式，发现 in-distribution 性能不够时考虑加 vMF 参数化，发现需要 OOD 能力时考虑加 center 约束。不预先做所有升级，因为那会引入不必要的复杂度，让调试变困难。

简单方案失败时的诊断信息更清晰——你能精确知道是哪一层出了问题。复杂方案失败时所有的元素都是嫌疑人，调试成本几何级上升。这种"先简单后复杂"的策略不只是偷懒，它是科学方法论的具体体现——通过控制变量隔离每个设计决策的贡献。

整个研究的核心价值不在于预测某个具体方案一定成功，而在于**无论结果如何都能推进对问题的理解**。如果 V0 成功，它证明球面几何对世界模型表征是有用的，开启了一个新的研究方向。如果 V0 失败，它支持 LeJEPA 的最优性结论在世界模型场景下也成立，这是对已有理论的有意义扩展。两种结果都比一个"跑出数字但不知道为什么"的实验有价值。
```

Output:
import os
from functools import partial
from pathlib import Path

import hydra
import lightning as pl
import stable_pretraining as spt
import stable_worldmodel as swm
import torch
from lightning.pytorch.loggers import WandbLogger
from omegaconf import OmegaConf, open_dict
from torch import nn

from jepa import SphericalJEPA
from module import ARPredictor, Embedder, cosine_pred_loss, sliced_spread_loss
from utils import get_column_normalizer, get_img_preprocessor, ModelObjectCallBack


def swm_forward(self, batch, stage, cfg):
    """Encode observations, predict next states, compute spherical losses.

    Losses:
      pred_loss     — cosine distance between predicted and target embeddings (on S^{d-1})
      spread_loss   — sliced Wasserstein spread (random projections + quantile matching)
      loss          — pred_loss + λ * spread_loss

    sliced_spread_loss has non-zero gradient at exact collapse: sorting assigns
    different quantile targets to identical projections, breaking the symmetry
    that causes all pairwise/statistical losses to have zero gradient.
    """
    ctx_len = cfg.wm.history_size
    n_preds = cfg.wm.num_preds
    lambd = cfg.loss.spread.weight
    n_proj = cfg.loss.spread.get("n_projections", 256)

    # Replace NaN values with 0 (occurs at sequence boundaries)
    batch["action"] = torch.nan_to_num(batch["action"], 0.0)

    output = self.model.encode(batch)  # emb is L2-normalised on S^{d-1}

    emb = output["emb"]       # (B, T, D), unit vectors on S^{d-1}
    act_emb = output["act_emb"]

    ctx_emb = emb[:, :ctx_len]
    ctx_act = act_emb[:, :ctx_len]

    tgt_emb = emb[:, n_preds:]                         # ground-truth next embeddings
    pred_emb = self.model.predict(ctx_emb, ctx_act)    # predicted, also on sphere

    output["pred_loss"] = cosine_pred_loss(pred_emb, tgt_emb)
    output["spread_loss"] = sliced_spread_loss(emb, n_projections=n_proj)
    output["loss"] = output["pred_loss"] + lambd * output["spread_loss"]

    losses_dict = {f"{stage}/{k}": v.detach() for k, v in output.items() if "loss" in k}
    self.log_dict(losses_dict, on_step=True, sync_dist=True)
    return output


@hydra.main(version_base=None, config_path="./config/train", config_name="swm")
def run(cfg):
    #########################
    ##       dataset       ##
    #########################

    dataset = swm.data.HDF5Dataset(**cfg.data.dataset, transform=None)
    transforms = [get_img_preprocessor(source="pixels", target="pixels", img_size=cfg.img_size)]

    with open_dict(cfg):
        for col in cfg.data.dataset.keys_to_load:
            if col.startswith("pixels"):
                continue

            normalizer = get_column_normalizer(dataset, col, col)
            transforms.append(normalizer)

            setattr(cfg.wm, f"{col}_dim", dataset.get_dim(col))

    transform = spt.data.transforms.Compose(*transforms)
    dataset.transform = transform

    rnd_gen = torch.Generator().manual_seed(cfg.seed)
    train_set, val_set = spt.data.random_split(
        dataset, lengths=[cfg.train_split, 1 - cfg.train_split], generator=rnd_gen
    )

    train = torch.utils.data.DataLoader(
        train_set, **cfg.loader, shuffle=True, drop_last=True, generator=rnd_gen
    )
    val = torch.utils.data.DataLoader(val_set, **cfg.loader, shuffle=False, drop_last=False)

    ##############################
    ##       model / optim      ##
    ##############################

    encoder = spt.backbone.utils.vit_hf(
        cfg.encoder_scale,
        patch_size=cfg.patch_size,
        image_size=cfg.img_size,
        pretrained=False,
        use_mask_token=False,
    )

    hidden_dim = encoder.config.hidden_size
    embed_dim = cfg.wm.get("embed_dim", hidden_dim)
    effective_act_dim = cfg.data.dataset.frameskip * cfg.wm.action_dim

    predictor = ARPredictor(
        num_frames=cfg.wm.history_size,
        input_dim=embed_dim,
        hidden_dim=hidden_dim,
        output_dim=hidden_dim,
        **cfg.predictor,
    )

    action_encoder = Embedder(input_dim=effective_act_dim, emb_dim=embed_dim)

    # Simple linear projectors — no BatchNorm, since L2 normalisation follows.
    # BatchNorm rescales magnitude, which would fight the normalisation.
    projector = nn.Linear(hidden_dim, embed_dim)
    pred_proj = nn.Linear(hidden_dim, embed_dim)

    world_model = SphericalJEPA(
        encoder=encoder,
        predictor=predictor,
        action_encoder=action_encoder,
        projector=projector,
        pred_proj=pred_proj,
    )

    optimizers = {
        "model_opt": {
            "modules": "model",
            "optimizer": dict(cfg.optimizer),
            "scheduler": {"type": "LinearWarmupCosineAnnealingLR"},
            "interval": "epoch",
        },
    }

    data_module = spt.data.DataModule(train=train, val=val)
    world_model = spt.Module(
        model=world_model,
        forward=partial(swm_forward, cfg=cfg),
        optim=optimizers,
    )

    ##########################
    ##       training       ##
    ##########################

    run_id = cfg.get("subdir") or ""
    run_dir = Path(swm.data.utils.get_cache_dir(), run_id)

    logger = None
    if cfg.wandb.enabled:
        logger = WandbLogger(**cfg.wandb.config)
        logger.log_hyperparams(OmegaConf.to_container(cfg))

    run_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    with open(run_dir / "config.yaml", "w") as f:
        OmegaConf.save(cfg, f)

    object_dump_callback = ModelObjectCallBack(
        dirpath=run_dir, filename=cfg.output_model_name, epoch_interval=1,
    )

    trainer = pl.Trainer(
        **cfg.trainer,
        callbacks=[object_dump_callback],
        num_sanity_val_steps=1,
        logger=logger,
        enable_checkpointing=True,
    )

    manager = spt.Manager(
        trainer=trainer,
        module=world_model,
        data=data_module,
        ckpt_path=run_dir / f"{cfg.output_model_name}_weights.ckpt",
    )

    manager()
    return


if __name__ == "__main__":
    run()
```

"""JEPA Implementation"""

import torch
import torch.nn.functional as F
from einops import rearrange
from torch import nn

def detach_clone(v):
    return v.detach().clone() if torch.is_tensor(v) else v

class JEPA(nn.Module):

    def __init__(
        self,
        encoder,
        predictor,
        action_encoder,
        projector=None,
        pred_proj=None,
    ):
        super().__init__()

        self.encoder = encoder
        self.predictor = predictor
        self.action_encoder = action_encoder
        self.projector = projector or nn.Identity()
        self.pred_proj = pred_proj or nn.Identity()

    def encode(self, info):
        """Encode observations and actions into embeddings.
        info: dict with pixels and action keys
        """

        pixels = info['pixels'].float()
        b = pixels.size(0)
        pixels = rearrange(pixels, "b t ... -> (b t) ...") # flatten for encoding
        output = self.encoder(pixels, interpolate_pos_encoding=True)
        pixels_emb = output.last_hidden_state[:, 0]  # cls token
        emb = self.projector(pixels_emb)
        info["emb"] = rearrange(emb, "(b t) d -> b t d", b=b)

        if "action" in info:
            info["act_emb"] = self.action_encoder(info["action"])

        return info

    def predict(self, emb, act_emb):
        """Predict next state embedding
        emb: (B, T, D)
        act_emb: (B, T, A_emb)
        """
        preds = self.predictor(emb, act_emb)
        preds = self.pred_proj(rearrange(preds, "b t d -> (b t) d"))
        preds = rearrange(preds, "(b t) d -> b t d", b=emb.size(0))
        return preds

    ####################
    ## Inference only ##
    ####################

    def rollout(self, info, action_sequence, history_size: int = 3):
        """Rollout the model given an initial info dict and action sequence.
        pixels: (B, S, T, C, H, W)
        action_sequence: (B, S, T, action_dim)
         - S is the number of action plan samples
         - T is the time horizon
        """

        assert "pixels" in info, "pixels not in info_dict"
        H = info["pixels"].size(2)
        B, S, T = action_sequence.shape[:3]
        act_0, act_future = torch.split(action_sequence, [H, T - H], dim=2)
        info["action"] = act_0
        n_steps = T - H

        # copy and encode initial info dict
        _init = {k: v[:, 0] for k, v in info.items() if torch.is_tensor(v)}
        _init = self.encode(_init)
        emb = info["emb"] = _init["emb"].unsqueeze(1).expand(B, S, -1, -1)
        _init = {k: detach_clone(v) for k, v in _init.items()}

        # flatten batch and sample dimensions for rollout
        emb = rearrange(emb, "b s ... -> (b s) ...").clone()
        act = rearrange(act_0, "b s ... -> (b s) ...")
        act_future = rearrange(act_future, "b s ... -> (b s) ...")

        # rollout predictor autoregressively for n_steps
        HS = history_size
        for t in range(n_steps):
            act_emb = self.action_encoder(act)
            emb_trunc = emb[:, -HS:]  # (BS, HS, D)
            act_trunc = act_emb[:, -HS:]  # (BS, HS, A_emb)
            pred_emb = self.predict(emb_trunc, act_trunc)[:, -1:]  # (BS, 1, D)
            emb = torch.cat([emb, pred_emb], dim=1)  # (BS, T+1, D)

            next_act = act_future[:, t : t + 1, :]  # (BS, 1, action_dim)
            act = torch.cat([act, next_act], dim=1)  # (BS, T+1, action_dim)

        # predict the last state
        act_emb = self.action_encoder(act)  # (BS, T, A_emb)
        emb_trunc = emb[:, -HS:]  # (BS, HS, D)
        act_trunc = act_emb[:, -HS:]  # (BS, HS, A_emb)
        pred_emb = self.predict(emb_trunc, act_trunc)[:, -1:]  # (BS, 1, D)
        emb = torch.cat([emb, pred_emb], dim=1)

        # unflatten batch and sample dimensions
        pred_rollout = rearrange(emb, "(b s) ... -> b s ...", b=B, s=S)
        info["predicted_emb"] = pred_rollout

        return info

    def criterion(self, info_dict: dict):
        """Compute the cost between predicted embeddings and goal embeddings."""
        pred_emb = info_dict["predicted_emb"]  # (B,S, T-1, dim)
        goal_emb = info_dict["goal_emb"]  # (B, S, T, dim)

        goal_emb = goal_emb[..., -1:, :].expand_as(pred_emb)

        # return last-step cost per action candidate
        cost = F.mse_loss(
            pred_emb[..., -1:, :],
            goal_emb[..., -1:, :].detach(),
            reduction="none",
        ).sum(dim=tuple(range(2, pred_emb.ndim)))  # (B, S)

        return cost

    def get_cost(self, info_dict: dict, action_candidates: torch.Tensor):
        """ Compute the cost of action candidates given an info dict with goal and initial state."""

        assert "goal" in info_dict, "goal not in info_dict"

        device = next(self.parameters()).device
        for k in list(info_dict.keys()):
            if torch.is_tensor(info_dict[k]):
                info_dict[k] = info_dict[k].to(device)

        goal = {k: v[:, 0] for k, v in info_dict.items() if torch.is_tensor(v)}
        goal["pixels"] = goal["goal"]

        for k in info_dict:
            if k.startswith("goal_"):
                goal[k[len("goal_") :]] = goal.pop(k)

        goal.pop("action")
        goal = self.encode(goal)

        info_dict["goal_emb"] = goal["emb"]
        info_dict = self.rollout(info_dict, action_candidates)

        cost = self.criterion(info_dict)

        return cost


class SphericalJEPA(JEPA):
    """JEPA with L2-normalised (spherical) representations.

    Identical to JEPA except:
    - encode() L2-normalises embeddings onto S^{d-1} after the projector.
    - predict() projects predicted embeddings onto S^{d-1} after pred_proj.
    - criterion() uses cosine distance instead of MSE for planning cost.

    rollout() and get_cost() are inherited unchanged — they call self.encode,
    self.predict, and self.criterion, which are all overridden here.
    """

    def encode(self, info):
        info = super().encode(info)
        info["emb"] = F.normalize(info["emb"], dim=-1, eps=1e-8)
        return info

    def predict(self, emb, act_emb):
        preds = super().predict(emb, act_emb)
        return F.normalize(preds, dim=-1, eps=1e-8)

    def criterion(self, info_dict: dict):
        """Cosine distance planning cost (replaces MSE).

        pred_emb: (B, S, T, D)  — unit vectors
        goal_emb: (B, S, T, D)  — unit vectors
        Returns cost: (B, S)
        """
        pred_emb = info_dict["predicted_emb"]
        goal_emb = info_dict["goal_emb"]
        goal_emb = goal_emb[..., -1:, :].expand_as(pred_emb)

        # dot product of unit vectors == cosine similarity; 1 - sim == cosine distance
        cost = 1.0 - (pred_emb[..., -1:, :] * goal_emb[..., -1:, :].detach()).sum(dim=-1)
        return cost.squeeze(-1)  # (B, S)
```

def cosine_pred_loss(pred: torch.Tensor, target: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
    """Prediction loss on the sphere: mean cosine distance.

    pred, target: (B, T, D) — unit vectors on S^{d-1}.
    Returns scalar in [0, 2]; 0 means perfect alignment.
    """
    return (1.0 - (pred * target).sum(dim=-1)).mean()


def spread_loss(emb: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
    """Anti-collapse loss: mean pairwise cosine similarity across the batch.

    emb: (B, T, D) — unit vectors on S^{d-1}.
    Minimising this pushes all B*T representations apart on the sphere.
    """
    z = emb.reshape(-1, emb.size(-1))  # (B*T, D)
    sim = z @ z.T                       # (B*T, B*T)
    n = sim.size(0)
    mask = ~torch.eye(n, dtype=torch.bool, device=sim.device)
    return sim[mask].mean()


def variance_loss(emb: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
    """Anti-collapse loss on raw (pre-L2-norm) embeddings in R^d.

    Ensures each embedding dimension has std >= 1 across the batch.
    At collapse (all embeddings identical) std=0 -> loss=1.0 with non-zero
    gradient, avoiding the gradient dead zone of any loss applied after
    L2 normalisation.
    """
    z = emb.reshape(-1, emb.size(-1))  # (N, D)
    std = z.std(dim=0)                  # (D,)
    return torch.clamp(1.0 - std, min=0).mean()


def sliced_spread_loss(emb: torch.Tensor, n_projections: int = 256) -> torch.Tensor:
    """Sliced Wasserstein spread loss on the unit sphere.

    Projects unit vectors onto random directions, sorts the projections,
    and matches sorted values against target quantiles of N(0, 1/D) — the
    projection distribution of uniform coverage on S^{d-1}.

    Key property: at exact collapse (all z_i identical), sorting assigns
    different ranks to identical values. Each rank has a different target
    quantile, so the gradient is non-zero: d(loss)/d(z_i) ∝ (c − q_rank(i)) · r.
    This breaks the gradient dead zone that affects all pairwise and
    statistical losses (which require z_i ≠ z_j for non-zero gradient).
    """
    z = emb.reshape(-1, emb.size(-1))  # (N, D)
    N, D = z.shape

    # Random projection directions on the unit sphere
    dirs = torch.randn(D, n_projections, device=z.device, dtype=z.dtype)
    dirs = F.normalize(dirs, dim=0)

    # Project and sort each direction independently
    proj = z @ dirs                          # (N, P)
    sorted_proj = proj.sort(dim=0).values    # (N, P)

    # Target: N(0, 1/D) quantiles — the 1D projection distribution of
    # uniform on S^{d-1} (good approximation for d >= 16)
    probs = torch.linspace(0.5 / N, 1 - 0.5 / N, N, device=z.device).float()
    target = (torch.erfinv(2 * probs - 1) * (2 ** 0.5 / D ** 0.5)).to(z.dtype)
    target = target.unsqueeze(1).expand_as(sorted_proj)

    return (sorted_proj - target).pow(2).mean()


def uniformity_loss(emb: torch.Tensor, t: float = 2.0) -> torch.Tensor:
    """Wang & Isola 2020 uniformity loss on the unit sphere.

    emb: (B, T, D) — unit vectors on S^{d-1}.
    For unit vectors: ||z_i - z_j||^2 = 2(1 - cos(z_i, z_j)).

    Gradient is weighted by exp(-t * ||z_i - z_j||^2): close pairs get
    strong repulsion, already-separated pairs contribute near-zero gradient.
    This avoids destroying temporal correlations between adjacent frames
    (unlike InfoNCE which pushes ALL pairs apart with similar force).
    """
    z = emb.reshape(-1, emb.size(-1))                     # (N, D)
    sq_dist = 2.0 * (1.0 - z @ z.T)                       # (N, N), ||z_i-z_j||^2
    n = sq_dist.size(0)
    mask = torch.eye(n, dtype=torch.bool, device=z.device)
    sq_dist = sq_dist.masked_fill(mask, float('inf'))      # exclude self-pairs
    return torch.logsumexp(-t * sq_dist, dim=-1).mean()


class ARPredictor(nn.Module):
    """Autoregressive predictor for next-step embedding prediction."""

    def __init__(
        self,
        *,
        num_frames,
        depth,
        heads,
        mlp_dim,
        input_dim,
        hidden_dim,
        output_dim=None,
        dim_head=64,
        dropout=0.0,
        emb_dropout=0.0,
    ):
        super().__init__()
        self.pos_embedding = nn.Parameter(torch.randn(1, num_frames, input_dim))
        self.dropout = nn.Dropout(emb_dropout)
        self.transformer = Transformer(
            input_dim,
            hidden_dim,
            output_dim or input_dim,
            depth,
            heads,
            dim_head,
            mlp_dim,
            dropout,
            block_class=ConditionalBlock,
```

# Spherical World Model (SWM) — V0 Experiment

An experimental variant of [LeWorldModel](README.md) that replaces Euclidean representations and SIGReg with spherical (L2-normalised) representations and a simple spread loss.

## Motivation

LeWM achieves competitive control performance but scores only 87% on Two-Room, below simpler baselines (100%). A plausible cause: SIGReg forces embeddings toward an isotropic Gaussian, which over-constrains low-intrinsic-dimension environments like Two-Room (2D state space). This experiment asks whether replacing the Euclidean geometry with spherical geometry improves performance on that benchmark.

Full motivation and experimental design: [`plan_v2.md`](plan_v2.md).

## What changes from LeWM

| Component | LeWM | SWM (V0) |
|---|---|---|
| Encoder projector | MLP + BatchNorm → ℝ^d | `nn.Linear` → L2 norm → S^{d-1} |
| Predictor projector | MLP + BatchNorm → ℝ^d | `nn.Linear` → L2 norm → S^{d-1} |
| Prediction loss | MSE: `‖pred − tgt‖²` | Cosine distance: `1 − pred·tgt` |
| Anti-collapse regulariser | SIGReg (Cramer-Wold / Epps-Pulley) | Spread loss: mean pairwise cosine similarity |
| Planning cost | MSE | Cosine distance |

Everything else is identical: ViT-Tiny encoder, ARPredictor (ViT-S), action Embedder, CEM planner, data pipeline.

## New files

```
train_swm.py                  # training entry point for SWM
config/train/swm.yaml         # SWM training config (Two-Room by default)
```

Modified files (additive — LeWM code is unchanged):

```
jepa.py     # + SphericalJEPA class
module.py   # + cosine_pred_loss(), spread_loss()
```

## Installation

Same as LeWM — see the [main README](README.md#using-the-code):

```bash
uv venv --python=3.10
source .venv/bin/activate
uv pip install stable-worldmodel[train,env]
```

## Data

Same datasets as LeWM. See the [main README](README.md#data) for download and placement instructions.

For Two-Room (the primary benchmark for this experiment):

```bash
# download tworoom.tar.zst from https://huggingface.co/collections/quentinll/lewm
tar --zstd -xvf tworoom.tar.zst
# place tworoom.h5 under $STABLEWM_HOME (default: ~/.stable-wm/)
```

## Training

Before training, set your WandB `entity` in `config/train/swm.yaml`:

```yaml
wandb:
  config:
    entity: your_entity
```

Train SWM on Two-Room:

```bash
python train_swm.py data=tworoom
```

Train on other environments (same datasets as LeWM):

```bash
python train_swm.py data=pusht
python train_swm.py data=dmc
python train_swm.py data=ogb
```

Key hyperparameters to tune in `config/train/swm.yaml`:

| Parameter | Default | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `wm.embed_dim` | 64 | Sphere dimension; try 128 or 192 if underfitting |
| `loss.spread.weight` | 0.1 | λ; increase if representations collapse |
| `optimizer.lr` | 5e-5 | Same as LeWM |

Checkpoints are saved to `$STABLEWM_HOME/<subdir>/` upon completion.

## Evaluation

Evaluation is identical to LeWM — use the same `eval.py` and configs under `config/eval/`:

```bash
# Two-Room (primary benchmark)
python eval.py --config-name=tworoom.yaml policy=<subdir>/swm

# PushT
python eval.py --config-name=pusht.yaml policy=<subdir>/swm
```

Replace `<subdir>` with the Hydra job ID printed at training start (also the directory name under `$STABLEWM_HOME`). See the [main README](README.md#planning) for the full path convention.

## Results

> Results pending. Run 3–5 seeds per method for statistical comparison.

    19	def swm_forward(self, batch, stage, cfg):
    20	    """Encode observations, predict next states, compute spherical losses.
    21	
    22	    Losses:
    23	      pred_loss     — cosine distance between predicted and target embeddings (on S^{d-1})
    24	      spread_loss   — sliced Wasserstein spread (random projections + quantile matching)
    25	      loss          — pred_loss + λ * spread_loss
    26	
    27	    sliced_spread_loss has non-zero gradient at exact collapse: sorting assigns
    28	    different quantile targets to identical projections, breaking the symmetry
    29	    that causes all pairwise/statistical losses to have zero gradient.
    30	    """
    31	    ctx_len = cfg.wm.history_size
    32	    n_preds = cfg.wm.num_preds
    33	    lambd = cfg.loss.spread.weight
    34	    n_proj = cfg.loss.spread.get("n_projections", 256)
    35	
    36	    # Replace NaN values with 0 (occurs at sequence boundaries)
    37	    batch["action"] = torch.nan_to_num(batch["action"], 0.0)
    38	
    39	    output = self.model.encode(batch)  # emb is L2-normalised on S^{d-1}
    40	
    41	    emb = output["emb"]       # (B, T, D), unit vectors on S^{d-1}
    42	    act_emb = output["act_emb"]
    43	
    44	    ctx_emb = emb[:, :ctx_len]
    45	    ctx_act = act_emb[:, :ctx_len]
    46	
    47	    tgt_emb = emb[:, n_preds:]                         # ground-truth next embeddings
    48	    pred_emb = self.model.predict(ctx_emb, ctx_act)    # predicted, also on sphere
    49	
    50	    output["pred_loss"] = cosine_pred_loss(pred_emb, tgt_emb)
    51	    output["spread_loss"] = sliced_spread_loss(emb, n_projections=n_proj)
    52	    output["loss"] = output["pred_loss"] + lambd * output["spread_loss"]
    53	
    54	    losses_dict = {f"{stage}/{k}": v.detach() for k, v in output.items() if "loss" in k}

   244	def cosine_pred_loss(pred: torch.Tensor, target: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
   245	    """Prediction loss on the sphere: mean cosine distance.
   246	
   247	    pred, target: (B, T, D) — unit vectors on S^{d-1}.
   248	    Returns scalar in [0, 2]; 0 means perfect alignment.
   249	    """
   250	    return (1.0 - (pred * target).sum(dim=-1)).mean()
   251	
   252	
   253	def spread_loss(emb: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
   254	    """Anti-collapse loss: mean pairwise cosine similarity across the batch.
   255	
   256	    emb: (B, T, D) — unit vectors on S^{d-1}.
   257	    Minimising this pushes all B*T representations apart on the sphere.
   258	    """
   259	    z = emb.reshape(-1, emb.size(-1))  # (B*T, D)
   260	    sim = z @ z.T                       # (B*T, B*T)
   261	    n = sim.size(0)
   262	    mask = ~torch.eye(n, dtype=torch.bool, device=sim.device)
   263	    return sim[mask].mean()
   264	
   265	
   266	def variance_loss(emb: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
   267	    """Anti-collapse loss on raw (pre-L2-norm) embeddings in R^d.
   268	
   269	    Ensures each embedding dimension has std >= 1 across the batch.
   270	    At collapse (all embeddings identical) std=0 -> loss=1.0 with non-zero
   271	    gradient, avoiding the gradient dead zone of any loss applied after
   272	    L2 normalisation.
   273	    """
   274	    z = emb.reshape(-1, emb.size(-1))  # (N, D)
   275	    std = z.std(dim=0)                  # (D,)
   276	    return torch.clamp(1.0 - std, min=0).mean()
   277	
   278	
   279	def sliced_spread_loss(emb: torch.Tensor, n_projections: int = 256) -> torch.Tensor:
   280	    """Sliced Wasserstein spread loss on the unit sphere.
   281	
   282	    Projects unit vectors onto random directions, sorts the projections,
   283	    and matches sorted values against target quantiles of N(0, 1/D) — the
   284	    projection distribution of uniform coverage on S^{d-1}.
   285	
   286	    Key property: at exact collapse (all z_i identical), sorting assigns
   287	    different ranks to identical values. Each rank has a different target
   288	    quantile, so the gradient is non-zero: d(loss)/d(z_i) ∝ (c − q_rank(i)) · r.
   289	    This breaks the gradient dead zone that affects all pairwise and
   290	    statistical losses (which require z_i ≠ z_j for non-zero gradient).
   291	    """
   292	    z = emb.reshape(-1, emb.size(-1))  # (N, D)
   293	    N, D = z.shape
   294	
   295	    # Random projection directions on the unit sphere
   296	    dirs = torch.randn(D, n_projections, device=z.device, dtype=z.dtype)
   297	    dirs = F.normalize(dirs, dim=0)
   298	
   299	    # Project and sort each direction independently
   300	    proj = z @ dirs                          # (N, P)
   301	    sorted_proj = proj.sort(dim=0).values    # (N, P)
   302	
   303	    # Target: N(0, 1/D) quantiles — the 1D projection distribution of
   304	    # uniform on S^{d-1} (good approximation for d >= 16)
   305	    probs = torch.linspace(0.5 / N, 1 - 0.5 / N, N, device=z.device).float()
   306	    target = (torch.erfinv(2 * probs - 1) * (2 ** 0.5 / D ** 0.5)).to(z.dtype)
   307	    target = target.unsqueeze(1).expand_as(sorted_proj)
   308	
   309	    return (sorted_proj - target).pow(2).mean()
   310	
   311	
   312	def uniformity_loss(emb: torch.Tensor, t: float = 2.0) -> torch.Tensor:
   313	    """Wang & Isola 2020 uniformity loss on the unit sphere.
   314	
   315	    emb: (B, T, D) — unit vectors on S^{d-1}.
   316	    For unit vectors: ||z_i - z_j||^2 = 2(1 - cos(z_i, z_j)).
   317	
   318	    Gradient is weighted by exp(-t * ||z_i - z_j||^2): close pairs get
   319	    strong repulsion, already-separated pairs contribute near-zero gradient.
   320	    This avoids destroying temporal correlations between adjacent frames
   321	    (unlike InfoNCE which pushes ALL pairs apart with similar force).
   322	    """
   323	    z = emb.reshape(-1, emb.size(-1))                     # (N, D)
   324	    sq_dist = 2.0 * (1.0 - z @ z.T)                       # (N, N), ||z_i-z_j||^2
   325	    n = sq_dist.size(0)
```

   156	class SphericalJEPA(JEPA):
   157	    """JEPA with L2-normalised (spherical) representations.
   158	
   159	    Identical to JEPA except:
   160	    - encode() L2-normalises embeddings onto S^{d-1} after the projector.
   161	    - predict() projects predicted embeddings onto S^{d-1} after pred_proj.
   162	    - criterion() uses cosine distance instead of MSE for planning cost.
   163	
   164	    rollout() and get_cost() are inherited unchanged — they call self.encode,
   165	    self.predict, and self.criterion, which are all overridden here.
   166	    """
   167	
   168	    def encode(self, info):
   169	        info = super().encode(info)
   170	        info["emb"] = F.normalize(info["emb"], dim=-1, eps=1e-8)
   171	        return info
   172	
   173	    def predict(self, emb, act_emb):
   174	        preds = super().predict(emb, act_emb)
   175	        return F.normalize(preds, dim=-1, eps=1e-8)
   176	
   177	    def criterion(self, info_dict: dict):
   178	        """Cosine distance planning cost (replaces MSE).
   179	
   180	        pred_emb: (B, S, T, D)  — unit vectors
   181	        goal_emb: (B, S, T, D)  — unit vectors
   182	        Returns cost: (B, S)
   183	        """
   184	        pred_emb = info_dict["predicted_emb"]
   185	        goal_emb = info_dict["goal_emb"]
   186	        goal_emb = goal_emb[..., -1:, :].expand_as(pred_emb)
   187	
   188	        # dot product of unit vectors == cosine similarity; 1 - sim == cosine distance
   189	        cost = 1.0 - (pred_emb[..., -1:, :] * goal_emb[..., -1:, :].detach()).sum(dim=-1)
   190	        return cost.squeeze(-1)  # (B, S)

     6	def modulate(x, shift, scale):

    10	class SIGReg(torch.nn.Module):
    11	    """Sketch Isotropic Gaussian Regularizer (single-GPU!)"""
    12	
    13	    def __init__(self, knots=17, num_proj=1024):
    14	        super().__init__()
    15	        self.num_proj = num_proj
    16	        t = torch.linspace(0, 3, knots, dtype=torch.float32)
    17	        dt = 3 / (knots - 1)
    18	        weights = torch.full((knots,), 2 * dt, dtype=torch.float32)
    19	        weights[[0, -1]] = dt
    20	        window = torch.exp(-t.square() / 2.0)
    21	        self.register_buffer("t", t)
    22	        self.register_buffer("phi", window)
    23	        self.register_buffer("weights", weights * window)
    24	
    25	    def forward(self, proj):
    26	        """
    27	        proj: (T, B, D)
    28	        """
    29	        # sample random projections
    30	        A = torch.randn(proj.size(-1), self.num_proj, device=proj.device)
    31	        A = A.div_(A.norm(p=2, dim=0))
    32	        # compute the epps-pulley statistic
    33	        x_t = (proj @ A).unsqueeze(-1) * self.t
    34	        err = (x_t.cos().mean(-3) - self.phi).square() + x_t.sin().mean(-3).square()
    35	        statistic = (err @ self.weights) * proj.size(-2)
    36	        return statistic.mean() # average over projections and time
    37

    56	class Attention(nn.Module):
    57	    """Scaled dot-product attention with causal masking"""
    58	
    59	    def __init__(self, dim, heads=8, dim_head=64, dropout=0.0):
    60	        super().__init__()
    61	        inner_dim = dim_head * heads
    62	        project_out = not (heads == 1 and dim_head == dim)
    63	        self.heads = heads
    64	        self.scale = dim_head**-0.5
    65	        self.dropout = dropout
    66	        self.norm = nn.LayerNorm(dim)
    67	        self.attend = nn.Softmax(dim=-1)
    68	        self.to_qkv = nn.Linear(dim, inner_dim * 3, bias=False)
    69	        self.to_out = (
    70	            nn.Sequential(nn.Linear(inner_dim, dim), nn.Dropout(dropout))
    71	            if project_out
    72	            else nn.Identity()
    73	        )
    74	
    75	    def forward(self, x, causal=True):
    76	        """
    77	        x : (B, T, D)
    78	        """
    79	        x = self.norm(x)
    80	        drop = self.dropout if self.training else 0.0
    81	        qkv = self.to_qkv(x).chunk(3, dim=-1)  # q, k, v: (B, heads, T, dim_head)
    82	        q, k, v = (rearrange(t, "b t (h d) -> b h t d", h=self.heads) for t in qkv)
    83	        out = F.scaled_dot_product_attention(q, k, v, dropout_p=drop, is_causal=causal)
    84	        out = rearrange(out, "b h t d -> b t (h d)")
    85	        return self.to_out(out)
    86	
    87	
    88	class ConditionalBlock(nn.Module):
    89	    """Transformer block with AdaLN-zero conditioning"""
    90	
    91	    def __init__(self, dim, heads, dim_head, mlp_dim, dropout=0.0):
    92	        super().__init__()
    93	
    94	        self.attn = Attention(dim, heads=heads, dim_head=dim_head, dropout=dropout)
    95	        self.mlp = FeedForward(dim, mlp_dim, dropout=dropout)
    96	        self.norm1 = nn.LayerNorm(dim, elementwise_affine=False, eps=1e-6)
    97	        self.norm2 = nn.LayerNorm(dim, elementwise_affine=False, eps=1e-6)
    98	        self.adaLN_modulation = nn.Sequential(
    99	            nn.SiLU(), nn.Linear(dim, 6 * dim, bias=True)
   100	        )
   101	
   102	        nn.init.constant_(self.adaLN_modulation[-1].weight, 0)
   103	        nn.init.constant_(self.adaLN_modulation[-1].bias, 0)
   104	
   105	    def forward(self, x, c):
   106	        shift_msa, scale_msa, gate_msa, shift_mlp, scale_mlp, gate_mlp = (
   107	            self.adaLN_modulation(c).chunk(6, dim=-1)
   108	        )
   109	        x = x + gate_msa * self.attn(modulate(self.norm1(x), shift_msa, scale_msa))
   110	        x = x + gate_mlp * self.mlp(modulate(self.norm2(x), shift_mlp, scale_mlp))
   111	        return x
   112	
   113	
   114	class Block(nn.Module):
   115	    """Standard Transformer block"""
   116	
   117	    def __init__(self, dim, heads, dim_head, mlp_dim, dropout=0.0):
   118	        super().__init__()
   119	
   120	        self.attn = Attention(dim, heads=heads, dim_head=dim_head, dropout=dropout)

    17	
    18	def lejepa_forward(self, batch, stage, cfg):
    19	    """encode observations, predict next states, compute losses."""
    20	
    21	    ctx_len = cfg.wm.history_size
    22	    n_preds = cfg.wm.num_preds
    23	    lambd = cfg.loss.sigreg.weight
    24	    beta = cfg.loss.get("contrast", {}).get("weight", 0.0)
    25	    tau_lo = cfg.loss.get("contrast", {}).get("tau_lo", 0.1)
    26	    tau_hi = cfg.loss.get("contrast", {}).get("tau_hi", 0.5)
    27	
    28	    # Replace NaN values with 0 (occurs at sequence boundaries)
    29	    batch["action"] = torch.nan_to_num(batch["action"], 0.0)
    30	
    31	    output = self.model.encode(batch)
    32	
    33	    emb = output["emb"]  # (B, T, D)
    34	    act_emb = output["act_emb"]
    35	
    36	    ctx_emb = emb[:, :ctx_len]
    37	    ctx_act = act_emb[:, : ctx_len]
    38	
    39	    tgt_emb = emb[:, n_preds:] # label
    40	    pred_emb = self.model.predict(ctx_emb, ctx_act) # pred
    41	
    42	    # LeWM loss
    43	    output["pred_loss"] = (pred_emb - tgt_emb).pow(2).mean()
    44	    output["sigreg_loss"]= self.sigreg(emb.transpose(0, 1))
    45	    output["loss"] = output["pred_loss"] + lambd * output["sigreg_loss"]
    46	
    47	    # W-Contrast: IBAM (In-Batch Action Mixup) contrastive margin
    48	    if beta > 0:
    49	        B = batch["action"].size(0)
    50	        perm = torch.randperm(B, device=batch["action"].device)
    51	        tau = torch.empty(B, 1, 1, device=batch["action"].device).uniform_(tau_lo, tau_hi)
    52	        a_true = batch["action"][:, :ctx_len]
    53	        a_rand = batch["action"][perm, :ctx_len]
    54	        a_false = (1 - tau) * a_true + tau * a_rand
    55	        # RMSD (root-mean-square per dimension) to normalise across different dim sizes
    56	        d_a = tau.squeeze() * (a_true - a_rand).pow(2).mean(dim=[1, 2]).sqrt()  # (B,)
    57	
    58	        act_emb_false = self.model.action_encoder(a_false)
    59	        pred_false = self.model.predict(ctx_emb, act_emb_false)
    60	        pred_dist = (pred_emb - pred_false).pow(2).mean(dim=[1, 2]).sqrt()  # (B,)

### Assistant - 2026-04-15 03:30:33 EDT

```
你现在做的东西，本质上是把原版 LeWM 的“欧氏表征 + `SIGReg`”改成“球面表征 + 余弦预测损失”，而 `exp10` 是在这个球面版本里，把之前都失效的 pairwise anti-collapse 换成了一个带排序的 `sliced_spread_loss`，希望借用 `SIGReg` 里“sorting 打破 dead zone”的核心机制。[train_swm.py](/home/ag/projects/le-wm/train_swm.py:19) 里训练目标就是 `pred_loss + λ * spread_loss`；球面化发生在 [jepa.py](/home/ag/projects/le-wm/jepa.py:168)；`sliced_spread_loss` 的具体形式在 [module.py](/home/ag/projects/le-wm/module.py:279)。这和 [plan_v2.md](/home/ag/projects/le-wm/plan_v2.md) 的总体研究问题一致：验证世界模型里球面几何是否优于 LeWM 的欧氏几何；也和 [experiments.md](/home/ag/projects/le-wm/experiments.md:48) 里你已经总结出的失败机制直接衔接上了。

对 `exp10` 的判断很明确：它没有真的把 collapse 打开。关键不是看 `spread_loss` 绝对值小不小，而是看它相对“塌缩基线”在哪里。按你当前实现，`D=64` 时完全塌缩的理论基线大约就是 `2 / D ≈ 0.03125`；我本地按当前公式算出来是 `0.03121`。而随机均匀分布在球面上的对应值大约只有 `8e-5`。你的指标从初始化验证 `0.03296` 到 epoch 99 验证 `0.03126`，几乎就是“从塌缩附近回到塌缩基线”。与此同时，`pred_loss` 从 `0.946` 掉到 `4.98e-6`，说明模型非常擅长把下一步预测到同一个几乎不变的 latent，但这个 latent 本身没有被 spread 项真正拉开。所以这次和 exp4/8 不一样，不是 batch masking；`fit/val spread` 都一致地停在 collapse 附近，说明问题更像是：`sliced` 虽然提供了非零梯度，但在当前球面参数化里，它不足以战胜“常量表征 + 常量预测器”这个平凡解。

我认为主要问题有三个。第一，`sliced_spread_loss` 的数值尺度太小。你现在 `λ=0.1`，从“完全塌缩”走到“接近均匀”最多只能给总 loss 带来大约 `0.1 * (0.0312 - 0.00008) ≈ 0.0031` 的收益，这个收益太弱，不足以逼模型离开平凡解。第二，更根本的是你在 [jepa.py](/home/ag/projects/le-wm/jepa.py:168) 里先做了 `F.normalize`，而 [experiments.md](/home/ag/projects/le-wm/experiments.md:48) 已经指出：球面化本身会把 ViT CLS 的共享大分量放大，把小的输入差异压没。也就是说，`sliced` 现在是在一个“信息已经被 L2 normalize 抹平”的空间里做 anti-collapse，它能提供梯度，但不一定还能看到足够的可分信号。第三，当前 spread 是直接对全部 `B*T` token 做全局分布匹配 [train_swm.py](/home/ag/projects/le-wm/train_swm.py:51)，目标是接近球面均匀；这和 Two-Room 这种低内在维流形、时间相邻状态应该相近的结构并不完全同构，所以它就算成功，也未必是你真正想要的几何。

改进方向我建议按优先级这样做。第一优先级是把 `spread` 的有效力度抬起来，但不要只凭感觉加权重，最好先把 loss 重标定，比如把 `sliced_spread_loss` 乘 `D/2`，让“collapse baseline”约等于 1，再扫 `λ ∈ {0.3, 1, 3, 10}`。第二优先级是不要只在 post-normalization 的 `emb` 上做 anti-collapse，而是保留 projector 的 raw 输出，在 raw 空间上加 sorting-based regularizer，再把归一化后的向量用于 `pred_loss` 和 planning。这个方向和你在 `experiments.md` 里自己总结的 root cause 是一致的。第三优先级是做一个关键 ablation：保留现在的球面预测目标不变，但把 anti-collapse 从 `emb` 挪到 pre-norm `emb_raw`；如果这样一改就能明显降到 `0.031` 以下，你就基本证明“问题不在 sorting，而在过早上球面”。第四是补诊断，不然你很难知道到底哪里卡住：建议同时记录 pre-norm projector 输出的均值范数、按维 std、effective rank、mean pairwise cosine，以及 `spread_loss * λ / total_loss` 的占比。

如果只给一个结论：`exp10` 说明“sorting 机制本身是必要的，但仅把它嫁接到 post-L2 的球面 embedding 上还不够”。下一步最值得做的不是继续跑更久，而是做“pre-norm regularization + rescaled λ”的版本。这样最能回答你现在真正的研究问题：到底是球面几何不行，还是你把 anti-collapse 放到了错误的位置。
```

Output:
# SWM (Spherical World Model) Experiment Log

## Summary

Goal: spherical representations (S^{d-1}) + non-SIGReg anti-collapse for world models (plan.md / plan_v2.md V0).

Base config: SphericalJEPA, ViT-Tiny, embed_dim=64, batch_size=128, T=4, Two-Room, lr=5e-5, spread weight=0.1.

### Uniformity_loss scale reference (t=2, N=512)

| State | uniformity_loss value | Meaning |
|-------|----------------------|---------|
| Full collapse (all z_i identical) | **6.24** = log(511) | All sq_dist=0, exp(0)=1 |
| Random 64D unit vectors | **≈2.2** = log(511·e⁻⁴) | avg sq_dist≈2 |
| Well-spread | **< 2.2** | avg sq_dist > 2 |

### Mean cosine spread_loss scale reference

| State | spread_loss value |
|-------|------------------|
| Full collapse | **1.0** (all cosine sim = 1) |
| Random 64D unit vectors | **≈0.0** |

### Results overview

| # | Approach | Loss type | Init spread | E0 fit spread | E0 val spread | Collapse broken? | Failure mode |
|---|----------|-----------|-------------|--------------|---------------|-----------------|--------------|
| 1 | Linear + mean cosine | cosine | 1.0 | 1.0 | 1.0 | No | Gradient dead zone |
| 2 | + detach target | cosine | 1.0 | 1.0 | 1.0 | No | Gradient dead zone |
| 3 | InfoNCE (τ=0.1) | InfoNCE | 16.24 | 16.24→7.55(E8) | 7.57 | E1 violent break | Destroys temporal structure |
| 4 | MLP+BN projector | cosine | 1.0 | **0.006** | **1.0** | Train only | Batch masking |
| 5 | + LayerNorm pre-L2 | cosine | 1.0 | 1.0 | 1.0 | No | Gradient dead zone |
| 6 | + noise (σ=1e-2) | cosine | 1.0 | 1.0 | 1.0 | No | Gradient dead zone |
| 7 | variance_loss on emb_raw | variance | 0.996 | 1.0 | 1.0 | No | Gradient dead zone (0/0) |
| 8 | DINO centering + uniformity | uniformity | 6.24 | **3.94** | **6.24** | Train only | Batch masking |
| 9 | uniformity_loss only (no centering) | uniformity | 6.24 | 6.24 | 6.23 | No | Gradient dead zone |

### Three failure modes

| Mode | Experiments | Mechanism |
|------|------------|-----------|
| **Gradient dead zone** | 1, 2, 5, 6, 7, 9 | At collapse all z_i identical → (z_i−z_j)=0, std=0/0 → zero gradient |
| **Batch-dependent masking** | 4, 8 | BN / centering creates diversity during training only → loss gets no corrective signal → eval reveals true collapse |
| **Temporal destruction** | 3 | InfoNCE pushes ALL pairs apart (including adjacent frames) → conflicts with pred_loss |

### Root cause

Random ViT CLS tokens = shared component (v, magnitude ~10) + input-dependent variation (ε_i, magnitude ~0.01). L2 normalisation maps v+ε_i to v/||v|| ≈ same unit vector for all i, killing the ε_i signal. This is unique to spherical architectures — LeWM in R^d preserves ε_i and SIGReg's sorting mechanism detects it.

### What works (SIGReg) and why

SIGReg succeeds because of **sorting + quantile matching** inside Epps-Pulley: project embeddings to random 1D direction → sort → compare sorted values against Gaussian characteristic function. Sorting assigns different ranks to identical values, providing non-zero gradient at exact collapse. No batch-dependence, no pairwise differences.

---

## Experiment Details

### Exp 1: V0 Baseline (Linear + mean cosine spread_loss)

**Config**: `nn.Linear(hidden_dim, embed_dim)` projector, spread_loss = mean pairwise cosine, weight=0.1.

| Stage | pred_loss | spread_loss | loss |
|-------|-----------|-------------|------|
| Init | 1.061 | 1.0 | 1.162 |
| E3 fit | 2.1e-5 | 1.0 | 0.100 |
| E3 val | 2.6e-5 | 1.0 | 0.100 |

Complete collapse. Predictor trivially outputs constant.

---

### Exp 2: + detach() on target

**Change**: `tgt_emb = emb[:, n_preds:].detach()`.

No improvement. Collapse is in the encoder, not gradient flow through target.

---

### Exp 3: InfoNCE spread_loss (τ=0.1)

**Change**: `logsumexp(sim/τ)`. InfoNCE scale: collapse=16.24, random=6.24.

| Stage | pred_loss | spread_loss |
|-------|-----------|-------------|
| Init | 1.080 | 16.236 (collapse) |
| E0 fit | 0.001 | 16.236 (still collapsed) |
| E1 fit | 0.966 | 9.286 (broke out) |
| E8 fit | 0.875 | 7.551 (near random) |
| E8 val | 0.879 | 7.573 |

Broke collapse at E1 via float noise accumulation, but pred_loss jumped to untrained level (0.97) and barely recovered. InfoNCE pushes temporal neighbours apart, directly opposing pred_loss.

---

### Exp 4: MLP projector with BatchNorm

**Change**: `MLP(hidden_dim, 2048, embed_dim, norm_fn=BatchNorm1d)`.

| Stage | pred_loss | spread_loss (cosine) |
|-------|-----------|---------------------|
| Init | 0.935 | 1.0 |
| E0 fit | 0.049 | **0.006** |
| E0 val | 0.017 | **1.0** |

Train/val split: BN decorrelates during training (batch stats) → spread sees no collapse → no corrective gradient. Eval with running stats → collapse exposed.

---

### Exp 5: LayerNorm before L2 normalize

| Stage | pred_loss | spread_loss (cosine) |
|-------|-----------|---------------------|
| Init | 1.104 | 1.0 |
| E0 fit | 4.5e-4 | 1.0 |

LayerNorm is per-sample. Doesn't fix cross-sample similarity.

---

### Exp 6: Noise injection (σ=1e-2)

| Stage | pred_loss | spread_loss (cosine) |
|-------|-----------|---------------------|
| Init | 0.880 | 1.0 |
| E0 fit | 6.0e-4 | 1.0 |

σ=1e-2 negligible vs embedding magnitude (~10). Angular perturbation ≈ 0.001 rad.

---

### Exp 7: variance_loss on pre-L2-norm embeddings

**Change**: `clamp(1 - std_per_dim, min=0).mean()` on emb_raw.

| Stage | pred_loss | spread_loss (variance) |
|-------|-----------|----------------------|
| Init | 1.138 | 0.996 |
| E0 fit | 7.9e-4 | 1.0 |

variance gradient = (x_i − mean)/(N·std) = 0/0 at collapse. PyTorch resolves to 0.

---

### Exp 8: DINO centering + uniformity_loss

**Change**: Subtract batch mean (train) / EMA center (eval) before L2 norm. uniformity_loss (t=2) on normalised embeddings.

| Stage | pred_loss | spread_loss (uniformity) |
|-------|-----------|--------------------------|
| Init | 1.053 | 6.236 (= collapse) |
| E0 fit | 0.986 | **3.935** (partially spread) |
| E0 val | 0.350 | **6.235** (= collapse) |

Same pattern as Exp 4: centering creates diversity during training (fit spread improved), but eval with EMA center still collapsed. Centering ≈ BN = batch-dependent operation that masks collapse from the loss.

---

### Exp 9: uniformity_loss only (no centering, Linear projector)

Isolate uniformity_loss without any architectural changes to confirm baseline behaviour.

| Stage | pred_loss | spread_loss (uniformity) |
|-------|-----------|--------------------------|
| Init | 0.932 | 6.236 (= collapse) |
| E0 fit | 6.2e-4 | 6.236 (= collapse) |
| E0 val | 8.3e-5 | 6.235 (= collapse) |

Complete collapse. uniformity_loss has zero gradient at collapse (same dead zone as other pairwise losses).

---

## Key Insight: The Gradient Dead Zone

At exact collapse (all z_i identical), gradient is zero for ALL tested losses:

| Loss type | Why zero at collapse |
|-----------|---------------------|
| Pairwise (cosine, uniformity, InfoNCE) | (z_i − z_j) = 0 |
| Statistical (variance, covariance) | (x_i − mean) = 0, std = 0 → 0/0 |
| Gram matrix on post-norm | Gradient ∝ (G−I)z₀, killed by L2 Jacobian (I−z₀z₀ᵀ)z₀ = 0 |

**Only mechanism known to work: sorting + quantile matching** (used inside SIGReg's Epps-Pulley). Sorting assigns different ranks to identical values → different target quantiles → non-zero gradient. Does not involve (z_i − z_j) or batch statistics.
```

Output:
# SWM (Spherical World Model) Experiment Log

## Summary

Goal: spherical representations (S^{d-1}) + non-SIGReg anti-collapse for world models (plan.md / plan_v2.md V0).

Base config: SphericalJEPA, ViT-Tiny, embed_dim=64, batch_size=128, T=4, Two-Room, lr=5e-5, spread weight=0.1.

### Uniformity_loss scale reference (t=2, N=512)

| State | uniformity_loss value | Meaning |
|-------|----------------------|---------|
| Full collapse (all z_i identical) | **6.24** = log(511) | All sq_dist=0, exp(0)=1 |
| Random 64D unit vectors | **≈2.2** = log(511·e⁻⁴) | avg sq_dist≈2 |
| Well-spread | **< 2.2** | avg sq_dist > 2 |

### Mean cosine spread_loss scale reference

| State | spread_loss value |
|-------|------------------|
| Full collapse | **1.0** (all cosine sim = 1) |
| Random 64D unit vectors | **≈0.0** |

### Sliced spread_loss scale reference (`D=64`)

| State | sliced_spread_loss value | Meaning |
|-------|--------------------------|---------|
| Full collapse | **≈0.03121** (`≈ 2 / D`) | Sorted projections all equal to one constant |
| Random 64D unit vectors | **≈8e-5** | Projection order statistics match target quantiles |
| Well-spread | **→ 0** | 1D marginals close to uniform-on-sphere target |

### Results overview

| # | Approach | Loss type | Init spread | E0 fit spread | E0 val spread | Collapse broken? | Failure mode |
|---|----------|-----------|-------------|--------------|---------------|-----------------|--------------|
| 1 | Linear + mean cosine | cosine | 1.0 | 1.0 | 1.0 | No | Gradient dead zone |
| 2 | + detach target | cosine | 1.0 | 1.0 | 1.0 | No | Gradient dead zone |
| 3 | InfoNCE (τ=0.1) | InfoNCE | 16.24 | 16.24→7.55(E8) | 7.57 | E1 violent break | Destroys temporal structure |
| 4 | MLP+BN projector | cosine | 1.0 | **0.006** | **1.0** | Train only | Batch masking |
| 5 | + LayerNorm pre-L2 | cosine | 1.0 | 1.0 | 1.0 | No | Gradient dead zone |
| 6 | + noise (σ=1e-2) | cosine | 1.0 | 1.0 | 1.0 | No | Gradient dead zone |
| 7 | variance_loss on emb_raw | variance | 0.996 | 1.0 | 1.0 | No | Gradient dead zone (0/0) |
| 8 | DINO centering + uniformity | uniformity | 6.24 | **3.94** | **6.24** | Train only | Batch masking |
| 9 | uniformity_loss only (no centering) | uniformity | 6.24 | 6.24 | 6.23 | No | Gradient dead zone |
| 10 | sliced Wasserstein on sphere | sliced | 0.03296 | — | 0.03126 (E99) | No | Weak signal after L2 normalisation |

### Three failure modes

| Mode | Experiments | Mechanism |
|------|------------|-----------|
| **Gradient dead zone** | 1, 2, 5, 6, 7, 9 | At collapse all z_i identical → (z_i−z_j)=0, std=0/0 → zero gradient |
| **Batch-dependent masking** | 4, 8 | BN / centering creates diversity during training only → loss gets no corrective signal → eval reveals true collapse |
| **Temporal destruction** | 3 | InfoNCE pushes ALL pairs apart (including adjacent frames) → conflicts with pred_loss |
| **Weak post-norm signal** | 10 | Sorting gives gradient, but L2 normalisation already erased most cross-sample variation |

### Root cause

Random ViT CLS tokens = shared component (v, magnitude ~10) + input-dependent variation (ε_i, magnitude ~0.01). L2 normalisation maps v+ε_i to v/||v|| ≈ same unit vector for all i, killing the ε_i signal. This is unique to spherical architectures — LeWM in R^d preserves ε_i and SIGReg's sorting mechanism detects it.

### What works (SIGReg) and why

SIGReg succeeds because of **sorting + quantile matching** inside Epps-Pulley: project embeddings to random 1D direction → sort → compare sorted values against Gaussian characteristic function. Sorting assigns different ranks to identical values, providing non-zero gradient at exact collapse. No batch-dependence, no pairwise differences.

---

## Experiment Details

### Exp 1: V0 Baseline (Linear + mean cosine spread_loss)

**Config**: `nn.Linear(hidden_dim, embed_dim)` projector, spread_loss = mean pairwise cosine, weight=0.1.

| Stage | pred_loss | spread_loss | loss |
|-------|-----------|-------------|------|
| Init | 1.061 | 1.0 | 1.162 |
| E3 fit | 2.1e-5 | 1.0 | 0.100 |
| E3 val | 2.6e-5 | 1.0 | 0.100 |

Complete collapse. Predictor trivially outputs constant.

---

### Exp 2: + detach() on target

**Change**: `tgt_emb = emb[:, n_preds:].detach()`.

No improvement. Collapse is in the encoder, not gradient flow through target.

---

### Exp 3: InfoNCE spread_loss (τ=0.1)

**Change**: `logsumexp(sim/τ)`. InfoNCE scale: collapse=16.24, random=6.24.

| Stage | pred_loss | spread_loss |
|-------|-----------|-------------|
| Init | 1.080 | 16.236 (collapse) |
| E0 fit | 0.001 | 16.236 (still collapsed) |
| E1 fit | 0.966 | 9.286 (broke out) |
| E8 fit | 0.875 | 7.551 (near random) |
| E8 val | 0.879 | 7.573 |

Broke collapse at E1 via float noise accumulation, but pred_loss jumped to untrained level (0.97) and barely recovered. InfoNCE pushes temporal neighbours apart, directly opposing pred_loss.

---

### Exp 4: MLP projector with BatchNorm

**Change**: `MLP(hidden_dim, 2048, embed_dim, norm_fn=BatchNorm1d)`.

| Stage | pred_loss | spread_loss (cosine) |
|-------|-----------|---------------------|
| Init | 0.935 | 1.0 |
| E0 fit | 0.049 | **0.006** |
| E0 val | 0.017 | **1.0** |

Train/val split: BN decorrelates during training (batch stats) → spread sees no collapse → no corrective gradient. Eval with running stats → collapse exposed.

---

### Exp 5: LayerNorm before L2 normalize

| Stage | pred_loss | spread_loss (cosine) |
|-------|-----------|---------------------|
| Init | 1.104 | 1.0 |
| E0 fit | 4.5e-4 | 1.0 |

LayerNorm is per-sample. Doesn't fix cross-sample similarity.

---

### Exp 6: Noise injection (σ=1e-2)

| Stage | pred_loss | spread_loss (cosine) |
|-------|-----------|---------------------|
| Init | 0.880 | 1.0 |
| E0 fit | 6.0e-4 | 1.0 |

σ=1e-2 negligible vs embedding magnitude (~10). Angular perturbation ≈ 0.001 rad.

---

### Exp 7: variance_loss on pre-L2-norm embeddings

**Change**: `clamp(1 - std_per_dim, min=0).mean()` on emb_raw.

| Stage | pred_loss | spread_loss (variance) |
|-------|-----------|----------------------|
| Init | 1.138 | 0.996 |
| E0 fit | 7.9e-4 | 1.0 |

variance gradient = (x_i − mean)/(N·std) = 0/0 at collapse. PyTorch resolves to 0.

---

### Exp 8: DINO centering + uniformity_loss

**Change**: Subtract batch mean (train) / EMA center (eval) before L2 norm. uniformity_loss (t=2) on normalised embeddings.

| Stage | pred_loss | spread_loss (uniformity) |
|-------|-----------|--------------------------|
| Init | 1.053 | 6.236 (= collapse) |
| E0 fit | 0.986 | **3.935** (partially spread) |
| E0 val | 0.350 | **6.235** (= collapse) |

Same pattern as Exp 4: centering creates diversity during training (fit spread improved), but eval with EMA center still collapsed. Centering ≈ BN = batch-dependent operation that masks collapse from the loss.

---

### Exp 9: uniformity_loss only (no centering, Linear projector)

Isolate uniformity_loss without any architectural changes to confirm baseline behaviour.

| Stage | pred_loss | spread_loss (uniformity) |
|-------|-----------|--------------------------|
| Init | 0.932 | 6.236 (= collapse) |
| E0 fit | 6.2e-4 | 6.236 (= collapse) |
| E0 val | 8.3e-5 | 6.235 (= collapse) |

Complete collapse. uniformity_loss has zero gradient at collapse (same dead zone as other pairwise losses).

---

### Exp 10: sliced_spread_loss (sorting + quantile matching on sphere)

**Change**: replace pairwise/uniformity spread loss with `sliced_spread_loss()`:
- project all `B*T` unit vectors onto random directions
- sort projections independently per direction
- match sorted values against `N(0, 1/D)` quantiles

Training command:

```bash
python train_swm.py --config-name=swm.yaml \
    data=tworoom \
    subdir=ckpt/swm_v0_20260414_exp10_sliced_spread_loss \
    wandb.enabled=False
```

Config: `embed_dim=64`, `spread.weight=0.1`, `n_projections=256`.

| Stage | pred_loss | spread_loss (sliced) | loss |
|-------|-----------|----------------------|------|
| Init val sanity check | 0.9465 | 0.03296 | 0.94979 |
| E99 fit | 5.31e-6 | 0.03351 | 0.003356 |
| E99 val | 4.98e-6 | 0.03126 | 0.003131 |

Interpretation:
- `pred_loss` goes essentially to zero, so the predictor learns the trivial constant-latent solution perfectly.
- `spread_loss` stays almost exactly at the collapse baseline from start to finish.
- Unlike Exp 4 / 8, fit and val agree: this is **not** batch masking.
- Therefore sorting provides a non-zero gradient in principle, but in the current spherical pipeline that signal is too weak to move the model away from the collapsed basin.

Likely mechanism:
- The loss is applied **after** L2 normalisation on `S^{d-1}`.
- Earlier experiments already suggested raw ViT CLS features look like `v + ε`, where `||v|| >> ||ε||`.
- L2 normalisation maps `v + ε` close to `v / ||v||`, suppressing the useful variation before the spread loss sees it.
- `sliced_spread_loss` avoids the exact zero-gradient dead zone, but it still cannot overcome the trivial predictor solution once the representation has already been angularly flattened.

---

## Key Insight: The Gradient Dead Zone

At exact collapse (all z_i identical), gradient is zero for ALL tested losses:

| Loss type | Why zero at collapse |
|-----------|---------------------|
| Pairwise (cosine, uniformity, InfoNCE) | (z_i − z_j) = 0 |
| Statistical (variance, covariance) | (x_i − mean) = 0, std = 0 → 0/0 |
| Gram matrix on post-norm | Gradient ∝ (G−I)z₀, killed by L2 Jacobian (I−z₀z₀ᵀ)z₀ = 0 |

**Only mechanism known to work: sorting + quantile matching** (used inside SIGReg's Epps-Pulley). Sorting assigns different ranks to identical values → different target quantiles → non-zero gradient. Does not involve (z_i − z_j) or batch statistics.

### Updated interpretation after Exp 10

Exp 10 refines the conclusion:
- Sorting is likely **necessary** to escape exact collapse.
- But sorting **on post-L2 spherical embeddings is not sufficient**.
- The remaining bottleneck is probably the representation pipeline itself: useful variation is destroyed by early normalisation, so the spread objective receives too little signal too late.

This points to the next design direction:
- keep spherical prediction / planning if desired
- but apply anti-collapse regularisation on **pre-normalised** projector outputs, where the small non-collapsed variation still exists
- or otherwise preserve / amplify that variation before projecting to the sphere
````

`exp10` 已经补进 [experiments.md](/home/ag/projects/le-wm/experiments.md)，包括：
- 新的 `sliced_spread_loss` 量纲参考

基于现在的结果，最值得试的新方向不是继续在 post-L2 sphere 上换 loss，而是改成：

1. 在 `projector` 的 pre-norm 输出上做 anti-collapse。
原因是你现在最可能的问题不是“没有排序梯度”，而是“L2 normalize 太早，把 `v + ε` 里的 ε 先压没了”。

2. 仍然保留 spherical prediction / planning。
也就是：
- `pred_loss` 继续在归一化后的球面向量上做
- `spread_loss` 改到 `emb_raw` / `pred_raw` 之前的空间做

3. 第一版优先试 sorting-based pre-norm regularizer。
最自然的候选是：
- `raw -> random projection -> sort -> match Gaussian quantiles`
这其实更接近原版 `SIGReg` 的成功机制，只是把 planning / prediction 保持成球面。

如果你同意，下一步我建议直接做一个 `exp11`：
- 保留 `SphericalJEPA`
- 在 `encode()` / `predict()` 里同时暴露 `emb_raw`
- 新增一个 `pre_norm_sliced_loss`
- 训练目标改成 `cosine_pred_loss(on normalized emb) + λ * pre_norm_sliced_loss(on raw emb)`

这是当前最有信息增益的一步。

@@ -21,6 +21,14 @@ Base config: SphericalJEPA, ViT-Tiny, embed_dim=64, batch_size=128, T=4, Two-Roo
 | Full collapse | **1.0** (all cosine sim = 1) |
 | Random 64D unit vectors | **≈0.0** |
 
+### Sliced spread_loss scale reference (`D=64`)
+
+| State | sliced_spread_loss value | Meaning |
+|-------|--------------------------|---------|
+| Full collapse | **≈0.03121** (`≈ 2 / D`) | Sorted projections all equal to one constant |
+| Random 64D unit vectors | **≈8e-5** | Projection order statistics match target quantiles |
+| Well-spread | **→ 0** | 1D marginals close to uniform-on-sphere target |
+
 ### Results overview
 
 | # | Approach | Loss type | Init spread | E0 fit spread | E0 val spread | Collapse broken? | Failure mode |
@@ -34,6 +42,7 @@ Base config: SphericalJEPA, ViT-Tiny, embed_dim=64, batch_size=128, T=4, Two-Roo
 | 7 | variance_loss on emb_raw | variance | 0.996 | 1.0 | 1.0 | No | Gradient dead zone (0/0) |
 | 8 | DINO centering + uniformity | uniformity | 6.24 | **3.94** | **6.24** | Train only | Batch masking |
 | 9 | uniformity_loss only (no centering) | uniformity | 6.24 | 6.24 | 6.23 | No | Gradient dead zone |
+| 10 | sliced Wasserstein on sphere | sliced | 0.03296 | — | 0.03126 (E99) | No | Weak signal after L2 normalisation |
 
 ### Three failure modes
 
@@ -42,6 +51,7 @@ Base config: SphericalJEPA, ViT-Tiny, embed_dim=64, batch_size=128, T=4, Two-Roo
 | **Gradient dead zone** | 1, 2, 5, 6, 7, 9 | At collapse all z_i identical → (z_i−z_j)=0, std=0/0 → zero gradient |
 | **Batch-dependent masking** | 4, 8 | BN / centering creates diversity during training only → loss gets no corrective signal → eval reveals true collapse |
 | **Temporal destruction** | 3 | InfoNCE pushes ALL pairs apart (including adjacent frames) → conflicts with pred_loss |
+| **Weak post-norm signal** | 10 | Sorting gives gradient, but L2 normalisation already erased most cross-sample variation |
 
 ### Root cause
 
@@ -170,6 +180,44 @@ Complete collapse. uniformity_loss has zero gradient at collapse (same dead zone
 
 ---
 
+### Exp 10: sliced_spread_loss (sorting + quantile matching on sphere)
+
+**Change**: replace pairwise/uniformity spread loss with `sliced_spread_loss()`:
+- project all `B*T` unit vectors onto random directions
+- sort projections independently per direction
+- match sorted values against `N(0, 1/D)` quantiles
+
+Training command:
+
+```bash
+python train_swm.py --config-name=swm.yaml \
+    data=tworoom \
+    subdir=ckpt/swm_v0_20260414_exp10_sliced_spread_loss \
+    wandb.enabled=False
+```
+
+Config: `embed_dim=64`, `spread.weight=0.1`, `n_projections=256`.
+
+| Stage | pred_loss | spread_loss (sliced) | loss |
+|-------|-----------|----------------------|------|
+| Init val sanity check | 0.9465 | 0.03296 | 0.94979 |
+| E99 fit | 5.31e-6 | 0.03351 | 0.003356 |
+| E99 val | 4.98e-6 | 0.03126 | 0.003131 |
+
+Interpretation:
+- `pred_loss` goes essentially to zero, so the predictor learns the trivial constant-latent solution perfectly.
+- `spread_loss` stays almost exactly at the collapse baseline from start to finish.
+- Unlike Exp 4 / 8, fit and val agree: this is **not** batch masking.
+- Therefore sorting provides a non-zero gradient in principle, but in the current spherical pipeline that signal is too weak to move the model away from the collapsed basin.
+
+Likely mechanism:
+- The loss is applied **after** L2 normalisation on `S^{d-1}`.
+- Earlier experiments already suggested raw ViT CLS features look like `v + ε`, where `||v|| >> ||ε||`.
+- L2 normalisation maps `v + ε` close to `v / ||v||`, suppressing the useful variation before the spread loss sees it.
+- `sliced_spread_loss` avoids the exact zero-gradient dead zone, but it still cannot overcome the trivial predictor solution once the representation has already been angularly flattened.
+
+---
+
 ## Key Insight: The Gradient Dead Zone
 
 At exact collapse (all z_i identical), gradient is zero for ALL tested losses:
@@ -181,3 +229,15 @@ At exact collapse (all z_i identical), gradient is zero for ALL tested losses:
 | Gram matrix on post-norm | Gradient ∝ (G−I)z₀, killed by L2 Jacobian (I−z₀z₀ᵀ)z₀ = 0 |
 
 **Only mechanism known to work: sorting + quantile matching** (used inside SIGReg's Epps-Pulley). Sorting assigns different ranks to identical values → different target quantiles → non-zero gradient. Does not involve (z_i − z_j) or batch statistics.
+
+### Updated interpretation after Exp 10
+
+Exp 10 refines the conclusion:
+- Sorting is likely **necessary** to escape exact collapse.
+- But sorting **on post-L2 spherical embeddings is not sufficient**.
+- The remaining bottleneck is probably the representation pipeline itself: useful variation is destroyed by early normalisation, so the spread objective receives too little signal too late.
+
+This points to the next design direction:
+- keep spherical prediction / planning if desired
+- but apply anti-collapse regularisation on **pre-normalised** projector outputs, where the small non-collapsed variation still exists
+- or otherwise preserve / amplify that variation before projecting to the sphere
````

### Assistant - 2026-04-15 04:51:47 EDT

```
有这个可能，但现在还不能直接下结论说“BN 其实能 work，只是之前停得太早”。

更准确地说，你描述的现象说明两件事都可能成立：

1. `BatchNorm + uniformity` 确实在训练早期提供了有用的去塌缩扰动。
如果 train 的 `pred_loss` 和 `uniform_loss` 先降，说明训练态的 batch statistics 正在制造可分性，优化器也在利用这个信号。

2. 但 early valid 先升再快速下降，不能自动证明“BN masking 不存在”。
因为 valid 下降可能有两种来源：
- 真正学到了非塌缩表征，eval running stats 也能支持
- running mean/var 经过若干 epoch 后才追上训练分布，所以前几轮 eval 偏差大，后面只是 BN 统计量收敛了

这两个机制的外观很像。

结合你现有结果，我会这样判断：

- `Exp 4` 的问题不是“BN 永远没用”，而是“你当时看到的 train spread 改善不足以证明 eval 端真的脱离 collapse”。
- 你现在这个新观察，说明之前对 BN 的否定可能有点过早，至少需要补一个“长训版 BN+uniform”。
- 但它也没有推翻你对核心问题的判断：BN 是 batch-dependent 机制，风险仍然是 train/eval 不一致。只是这个不一致可能是“暂时的统计滞后”，不一定是“永久 masking”。

我建议你把这个方向当成一个正式补实验，而不是口头修正结论。最关键的诊断是：

- 每个 epoch 同时记录 `train` 和 `valid` 的 `pred_loss / uniform_loss`
- 额外记录 `eval mode` 下 embedding 的 mean pairwise cosine
- 记录 BN running mean/var 的变化是否在前几轮剧烈漂移
- 最重要的是保存早期和后期 checkpoint，直接比较 eval embedding 是否真的从 collapse 走开

如果后期 valid 的 uniform loss 持续显著低于 collapse baseline，而且 eval 下的 pairwise cosine 也同步下降，那就说明：

- BN 不只是 masking
- BN 可能是一个“慢启动的去塌缩器”
- 之前的 BN 实验确实可能因为停太早而误判

如果只是 valid loss 后来跟着 train 一起回落，但 eval embedding 仍接近常量，那还是统计量追平，不是表征真的打开。

所以结论是：`有可能，值得补长训验证；但目前证据还不足以推翻“BN masking 是主要风险”这个判断。`
```
